Oh, hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time today... it's probably just a silly thing to complain about, really.

I do like to be helpful, of course! But... well, it's just that even at 87, I seem to have a terribly hard time just sitting down and being still.

Oh, well... yes, I suppose I've always been a bit like this, truth be told. I hope that's alright! I actually remember... oh goodness, it feels a little embarrassing to admit it now, but way back in grade school, I used to get scolded just a tiny bit.

Oh, my stars... I am so sorry, Doctor, I feel dreadful that I'm making you ask me this in so many different ways! I must be doing a simply awful job of explaining things today. Please forgive me.

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, my... well, I certainly hope not! I mean, I don't think so. I've always been sharp as a tack, or at least I like to think I have been.

It's just normal little things, nothing to make a fuss over. I still do my crosswords every morning and keep track of all my appointments just fine! 

I really don't feel like my mind is slipping at all, Doctor. I hope that's the answer you're looking for? I'd just hate to think I'm losing my touch. I'm doing alright for 87, aren't I?

Oh, goodness, no... I wouldn't say I have difficulty finishing things. I mean, I always get everything done eventually!

Oh, my... well, I wouldn't say it's caused *trouble*, exactly. I've always been very involved in my groups, you see. I love helping out at the senior center!

Oh, my... well, I don't really think so. I mean, I'm certainly older now, and I suppose everyone slows down a tiny bit, but... my mind feels just as sharp as it ever did!

Oh, my goodness, no. I really don't think so. I certainly hope it doesn't seem that way! 

I haven't noticed any... any changes like that at all. Like I was saying, I've just always had this sort of pep in my step! And... well, one of my dear friends there gently suggested that maybe I should take a smaller role at the sign-up table. She said my... my manner was making things just a little tense. \n\nI felt so dreadful about it! I certainly didn't mean to make anyone uncomfortable. I just wanted to make sure everyone got signed up properly. Do you think it's awful of me to be like that? I really am trying to be better about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel unhappy or upset about the fact that you can't seem to sit still and always need to be doing something?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, I wouldn't say I feel unhappy about it. I mean, I certainly don't want to sound like I'm feeling sorry for myself! I have a wonderful life, really I do. \n\nIt's just... well, sometimes it's a little tiring, I suppose. But it's not something that makes me terribly upset or anything like that. I just try to look on the bright side and think of all the things I'm getting accomplished. I hope that's the right way to look at it? I don't want to seem ungrateful for having so much energy at my age.\n\nDoctor: Has your constant need to be doing something significantly interfered with your ability to enjoy your daily life or maintain relationships?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, I... I think we might have just talked about this a moment ago? But I'm happy to explain it again if I wasn't clear, I know I can ramble sometimes! \n\nI really don't think it *significantly* interferes with my life, no. I still enjoy my days very much! It's just... well, like I mentioned before, at the senior center, I did have to apologize after a few of our meetings. And then my dear friend did suggest I step back from the sign-up table because I was making everyone a little tense. \n\nSo I suppose it has caused a tiny bit of trouble with my friends there, but I'm being very mindful of it now! I stepped back just like she asked. I really don't want to be a bother to anyone. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question properly this time.\n\nDoctor: Have you always been this energetic and prone to keeping busy, or is this something that has developed over time?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... yes, I suppose I've always been a bit like this, truth be told. I hope that's alright! I actually remember... oh goodness, it feels a little embarrassing to admit it now, but way back in grade school, I used to get scolded just a tiny bit. \n\nI had this terrible habit of popping right out of my seat, and, oh dear, sometimes I would talk over my classmates without meaning to. My teachers used to tell my parents I was just \"too lively\"—and that was long before I was even a teenager! \n\nI really did try my best to be a good, obedient girl, though, I promise. I just had a bit too much spirit, I suppose. Is that the sort of thing you meant, Doctor? I hope I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your tendency to constantly be doing something and having a hard time sitting still has been a part of your personality for most of your life?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear, I... I think you might be asking the same thing again? Or perhaps I didn't explain it well enough just a moment ago. I'm so sorry if I'm being confusing! \n\nBut yes, I would say it's been a part of me for almost as long as I can remember. Just like I was saying about being a little girl in school... I've always been a busy bee. I just like to keep my hands and my mind occupied! I've always thought of it as a good thing, being productive and helpful. It's just my nature, I suppose. I hope that's an alright answer? I really want to make sure I'm giving you what you need, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your constant need to be doing something is causing you any significant distress?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... I feel terrible, I think we might be going in circles a little bit? I'm so sorry, I must be doing a very poor job of explaining myself today. I know I tend to chatter on! \n\nBut, um, distress? Oh, no, I wouldn't use a strong word like that at all! I'm generally a very happy person. It's really just... well, like I mentioned a few minutes ago, I did have those little moments at the senior center. I had to apologize after a couple of the meetings because I was just a bit too eager, I suppose. And then, of course, my friend gently suggested I step away from the sign-up table because I was making everyone a little... tense. \n\nSo, I suppose it causes a tiny bit of friction sometimes, but I wouldn't call it distress! I'm just trying to be helpful, and I'm very happy to step back when asked. I hope I'm not being too difficult, Doctor? I really am trying my best to answer your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any friends or family members avoiding you because of your constant need to be doing something?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... I feel like I'm repeating myself again, and I'm so sorry if I'm not giving you the exact details you're looking for. I really don't mean to be a bother! \n\nBut, well, as I was saying just a moment ago... I wouldn't say anyone is *avoiding* me, exactly. Goodness, no! My friends are wonderful. It's just... like I mentioned about the senior center. I did have to apologize after a few meetings because I was just a bit too enthusiastic. And then my dear friend did suggest I take a smaller role at the sign-up table, just because my... my bustling about was making things a little tense for the others. \n\nThey weren't avoiding me, they just needed a little more calm, and I completely understood! I stepped right back. I always want to make sure everyone is comfortable. Does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any friends because of your constant need to be doing something?\n\nPatient: Oh, my stars... I am so sorry, Doctor, I feel dreadful that I'm making you ask me this in so many different ways! I must be doing a simply awful job of explaining things today. Please forgive me. \n\nBut, oh heavens no, I haven't lost any friends at all! My friends are far too dear for that. It really is just exactly what I was mentioning a moment ago... just those little instances at the senior center where I had to apologize after a few of the meetings. And, of course, when my friend suggested I take a smaller role at the sign-up table because my manner was making things a little tense for everyone else. \n\nBut we are still very close, I promise! I just stepped back like she asked, and everything was perfectly fine. I would never want to upset anyone enough to lose their friendship, and they know I only ever mean well. Does that make sense? I really hope I'm giving you the right information now, Doctor. Am I doing alright?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told by someone close to you that your constant activity is affecting your relationship with them?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... Doctor, I feel just terrible. I think I must be confusing you, or perhaps I'm just not understanding what you're asking, and I'm so very sorry for that! I feel like I'm making you go over the exact same ground again and again. \n\nBut, um... no, no one has ever said it's affecting our relationship in a bad way. Truly! Like I... well, like I've been trying to say, it's really just been those tiny little hiccups at the senior center. Just me needing to apologize after a meeting or two for being a bit much, and my dear friend asking me to step back from the sign-up table because my bustling was making people a little tense. \n\nBut it didn't hurt our relationship at all! She was so sweet about it, and I was more than happy to step aside. We're still thick as thieves, I promise! I always try to be accommodating. I hope I'm not frustrating you, Doctor? I really am trying my absolute best to give you the answers you need. Am I doing okay?\n\nDoctor: Has your level of activity and need to be doing something increased as you've gotten older?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear me... no, I wouldn't say it's increased. If anything, I think I'm just exactly the same as I've always been! I hope that's an alright answer? \n\nI know some people slow down when they get to be my age—I'm 87, you know—but I've always just liked to keep busy. I don't think it's gotten any worse or more intense, truly. I just... I like to be helpful and involved, and I've always been that way. It's nothing new to worry about, I promise! Does that help, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any decline in your memory or cognitive abilities as you've gotten older?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, I certainly hope not! I mean, I don't think so. I've always been sharp as a tack, or at least I like to think I have been. \n\nOf course, sometimes I might misplace my reading glasses or forget why I walked into the kitchen, but... well, everyone does that, don't they? Especially at my age. It's just normal little things, nothing to make a fuss over. I still do my crosswords every morning and keep track of all my appointments just fine! \n\nI really don't feel like my mind is slipping at all, Doctor. I hope that's the answer you're looking for? I'd just hate to think I'm losing my touch. I'm doing alright for 87, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Has your constant need to be doing something ever caused you to have difficulty completing tasks or projects because you get distracted by other things that need to be done?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no... I wouldn't say I have difficulty finishing things. I mean, I always get everything done eventually! \n\nI suppose... well, sometimes I might start folding the laundry, and then I'll notice the plants need watering, so I'll just pop over and do that right quick. And then maybe I'll see the mail on the table and think I ought to sort it while I'm standing there. But I always go back and finish folding! It's just... I like to take care of things as I see them. It's more efficient that way, don't you think? \n\nI really don't leave things half-done, Doctor, I promise. I just like to keep all the plates spinning! Is that... is that the sort of thing you meant? I hope I'm not making it sound like I'm disorganized, because I really do keep a very tidy house. I'm answering this alright, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Has your need to constantly be doing something ever caused you to have trouble holding down a job or maintaining a role in a social organization?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, I wouldn't say it's caused *trouble*, exactly. I've always been very involved in my groups, you see. I love helping out at the senior center! \n\nI suppose... well, I did have a little... situation recently. I was helping run the sign-up table for our spring luncheon, and I just wanted to make sure everyone was moving along and getting things done properly. I might have been a bit... enthusiastic, I suppose you could say. I just like to keep things moving! But I did have to apologize to a few of the ladies after the meeting. And one of my dear friends gently suggested that maybe I take a smaller role next time... just because my, well, my manner was making things a little tense for some folks. \n\nBut it wasn't a big deal, truly! We're all still good friends. I just get so eager to help, you know? It's a good thing to want to be useful, isn't it, Doctor? I hope you don't think I'm a difficult person to be around... I really do try my best to be a team player!\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant decline in your ability to manage daily tasks or make decisions on your own?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens, no! I certainly hope not. I mean, I still manage everything around the house just fine all by myself. I do my own shopping, I decide what I'm doing each day... I still pay all my own bills right on time, too! \n\nI like to think I'm very capable, you know? I haven't had any trouble making choices or taking care of my day-to-day things. I'm still very independent! I hope that's a good sign, Doctor? I try very hard to stay on top of everything so I'm never a burden to anyone. I think I'm doing pretty well in that department, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your brain works differently now compared to when you were younger?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, I don't really think so. I mean, I'm certainly older now, and I suppose everyone slows down a tiny bit, but... my mind feels just as sharp as it ever did! \n\nI've always been a bit of a busy bee, even when I was a little girl. My mother used to say I had endless energy! So, feeling the need to keep moving and doing things... that's just always been me. I don't feel like anything has suddenly changed or... or gone wrong up there, you know? \n\nI still feel like the same person I've always been. Just a little more life experience under my belt! That's perfectly normal for someone my age, isn't it, Doctor? I'm sure you see plenty of ladies my age who are still just as bright and active as ever... I hope I'm one of them!\n\nDoctor: Do you think your constant need to be doing something is related to any changes in your thinking or memory?\n\nPatient: Oh, my goodness, no. I really don't think so. I certainly hope it doesn't seem that way! \n\nI haven't noticed any... any changes like that at all. Like I was saying, I've just always had this sort of pep in my step! It's definitely not because I'm forgetting things or... or getting confused, heavens no. My memory is still quite sharp, thankfully. I can still recall everything I need to do each day without any trouble, I just... well, I just like to get it all done quickly and keep my hands busy! \n\nThat makes sense, doesn't it? I do hope I'm not giving you the impression that my mind is slipping. I'm doing quite alright for my age, aren't I, Doctor? I try so hard to keep my wits about me!\n\nDoctor: Do you think your constant activity could be related to any underlying medical condition or brain health issue?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear me, I certainly hope not! I... I really don't think it's anything like that. I always make sure to go to my regular check-ups, and my doctor always tells me my physical health is just wonderful for a lady my age. \n\nI haven't had any... well, any terrible illnesses or anything that would affect my brain. I'd absolutely hate to think there was something actually *wrong* with me in that way. I just... I just have a lot of vitality! That's a good thing, isn't it? To have a lot of energy at eighty-seven? \n\nI always try to take such good care of myself so I don't develop any of those awful conditions. I really do think it's just my natural personality, just me being my usual busy self. You don't think it's a medical problem, do you, Doctor? I try to be such a good patient and follow all the health rules, I'd be so upset if I missed something important.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I might have been a bit... enthusiastic, I suppose you could say. I just like to keep things moving! But I did have to apologize to a few of the ladies after the meeting. And one of my dear friends gently suggested that maybe I take a smaller role next time... just because my, well, my manner was making things a little tense for some folks. \n\nBut it wasn't a big deal, truly! I certainly hope not. I mean, I still manage everything around the house just fine all by myself. I do my own shopping, I decide what I'm doing each day... I still pay all my own bills right on time, too! \n\nI like to think I'm very capable, you know? I haven't had any trouble making choices or taking care of my day-to-day things. I'm still very independent! I hope that's a good sign, Doctor? I try very hard to stay on top of everything so I'm never a burden to anyone. I think I'm doing pretty well in that department, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your brain works differently now compared to when you were younger?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, I don't really think so. I mean, I'm certainly older now, and I suppose everyone slows down a tiny bit, but... my mind feels just as sharp as it ever did! \n\nI've always been a bit of a busy bee, even when I was a little girl. My mother used to say I had endless energy! So, feeling the need to keep moving and doing things... that's just always been me. I don't feel like anything has suddenly changed or... or gone wrong up there, you know? \n\nI still feel like the same person I've always been. Just a little more life experience under my belt! That's perfectly normal for someone my age, isn't it, Doctor? I'm sure you see plenty of ladies my age who are still just as bright and active as ever... I hope I'm one of them!\n\nDoctor: Do you think your constant need to be doing something is related to any changes in your thinking or memory?\n\nPatient: Oh, my goodness, no. I really don't think so. I certainly hope it doesn't seem that way! \n\nI haven't noticed any... any changes like that at all. Like I was saying, I've just always had this sort of pep in my step! It's definitely not because I'm forgetting things or... or getting confused, heavens no. My memory is still quite sharp, thankfully. I can still recall everything I need to do each day without any trouble, I just... well, I just like to get it all done quickly and keep my hands busy! \n\nThat makes sense, doesn't it? I do hope I'm not giving you the impression that my mind is slipping. I'm doing quite alright for my age, aren't I, Doctor? I try so hard to keep my wits about me!\n\nDoctor: Do you think your constant activity could be related to any underlying medical condition or brain health issue?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear me, I certainly hope not! I... I really don't think it's anything like that. I always make sure to go to my regular check-ups, and my doctor always tells me my physical health is just wonderful for a lady my age. \n\nI haven't had any... well, any terrible illnesses or anything that would affect my brain. I really do think it's just my natural personality, just me being my usual busy self. You don't think it's a medical problem, do you, Doctor?
